<h5 align="left"><a href="#toc">Table of Contents</a></h5>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><A NAME="tx274150_1a"></A>PART I </B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><A NAME="tx274150_1"></A>Item&nbsp;1. Business (All $ amounts are noted in thousands). </B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>General Overview </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Young Innovations, Inc. and its subsidiaries (&#147;the
Company&#148;) develops, manufactures and markets supplies and equipment used by dentists, dental hygienists, dental assistants and consumers.&nbsp;The Company&#146;s product offering includes disposable and metal prophylaxis (&#147;prophy&#148;)
angles, prophy cups and brushes, dental micro-applicators, panoramic X-ray machines, moisture control products, infection control products, dental handpieces (drills) and related components, endodontic systems, orthodontic toothbrushes, flavored
examination gloves, children&#146;s toothbrushes, and children&#146;s toothpastes. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company&#146;s manufacturing and distribution
facilities are located in Missouri, Illinois, California, Indiana, Wisconsin and Ireland. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company markets its products primarily in the
U.S.&nbsp;The Company also markets its products in several international markets, including Canada, Europe, South America, Central America and the Pacific Rim.&nbsp;Sales outside the United States represented approximately 17% of the Company&#146;s
total net sales in 2011. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company is a Missouri corporation with its corporate headquarters located at 13705 Shoreline Court East, Earth
City, Missouri 63045, in the St. Louis, Missouri metropolitan area; its telephone number is (314)&nbsp;344-0010.&nbsp;The telephone number for investor relations is&nbsp;(312) 644-4174. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Background </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company was founded as Young Dental in the early 1900s.&nbsp;As one
of many small suppliers to the dental profession, Young Dental&#146;s strength was manufacturing consistently reliable dental products.&nbsp;As dentistry evolved, Young Dental employees worked with practicing dentists and academics to identify
clinical problems.&nbsp;Young Dental staff used their engineering and manufacturing expertise to create solutions to those problems.&nbsp;Young Dental established a strong reputation and leading market position in disposable and metal prophy angles,
the core products utilized by the dentist in the typical biannual teeth cleaning treatment. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">In 1995, following the acquisition of The Lorvic
Corporation, the Company incorporated as Young Innovations, Inc. in the state of Missouri.&nbsp;Since then, the Company has acquired a number of complementary businesses, and introduced a variety of new products.&nbsp;Through these acquisitions and
new product introductions, the Company has expanded its preventive and infection control product offerings, and entered a number of new product areas, including dental diagnostic imaging, handpieces, home care products, and endodontics.&nbsp;The
Company believes its continued commitment to providing innovative products to meet the evolving needs of dental professionals has earned it a reputation for quality, reliability and value. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Business Strategy </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The key elements of the Company&#146;s strategy are: </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="font-size:6px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="5%"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">&#149;</FONT></TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Enhance and Expand Customer Relationships</I>.&nbsp;Enhancing and expanding customer relationships are fundamental components of the Company&#146;s
strategy.&nbsp;By understanding and responding to the needs of distributors, clinicians and patients, the Company seeks to serve a broader customer base by extending the reach of its existing products to new customers and new markets.
</FONT></P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="5%"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">&#149;</FONT></TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Improve Operating Efficiency</I>.&nbsp;The Company strives to reduce costs and rationalize expenses across its operations in an effort to maximize
profitability and better serve its customers.&nbsp;In order to realize operating efficiencies, the Company seeks cost savings through manufacturing and process improvements, administrative and marketing synergies, and strategic facility
consolidations. </FONT></P></TD></TR></TABLE>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">3 </FONT></P>

<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>New Product Development and Acquisitions.&nbsp;</I>The Company brings products to market through two methods: internal product development and
acquisitions. Internal product development focuses on evolutionary changes to its existing product lines to enhance patient care, increase patient comfort and improve practice productivity. The Company has typically entered new product categories
through acquisitions. The Company intends to pursue acquisition opportunities that increase the breadth of its product and service offerings, provide entrance into attractive new markets, and enhance the growth and profitability of the business. The

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Products </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company&#146;s $106,230 in sales for 2011 was derived from the manufacture and distribution of the products described below.&nbsp;The Company offers many different products which fall within one of
two general categories: consumables and diagnostics.&nbsp;Consumables consist of preventive, infection control, micro-applicators, home care and endodontic products.&nbsp;Diagnostics consist of X-ray machines and supplies.&nbsp;Revenue from
consumables was approximately $92,700, $91,600 and $88,400 for 2011, 2010 and 2009, respectively.&nbsp;Revenue from diagnostics was approximately $13,500, $11,200 and $9,400 for 2011, 2010 and 2009, respectively. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Consumables: </I></B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Preventive.&nbsp;</I></B>The Company believes it is a leading supplier of preventive products to the U.S. professional dental
market.&nbsp;Preventive products include: </FONT></P> <P STYLE="font-size:6px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="5%"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">&#149;</FONT></TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Prophy angles.&nbsp;The Company offers a broad line of prophy products.&nbsp;The prophy angle, in combination with prophy paste, is used in the typical
biannual teeth cleaning treatment that helps remove plaque and polishes teeth.&nbsp;The Company offers a variety of pre-assembled Classic and Contra disposable prophy angles with cups or brushes attached under both the Young Dental (premium-priced)
and Denticator (popular-priced) brand names, as well as through private-label relationships.&nbsp;The Company believes it is a leading U.S. manufacturer and distributor of disposable prophy angles, with its extensive and innovative line of prophy
products that satisfy a wide variety of customer needs and desires. </FONT></P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px; margin-left:13%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company also offers metal prophy
angles, which are sealed to help prevent damage to the internal components of the angle and help it withstand repeated sterilizations.&nbsp;The metal prophy angles are marketed together with an assortment of cups and brushes specifically designed to
work together, which encourages recurring purchases of these products. </FONT></P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">4 </FONT></P>

<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">
<TR>
<TD WIDTH="5%"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">&#149;</FONT></TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Prophy pastes.&nbsp;D-Lish, Festival and ProCare are some of the brand names of the Company&#146;s prophy pastes.&nbsp;Most pastes are available in a
variety of textures (grits) and flavors, and some are sold in powder form.&nbsp;Important functional features of prophy paste include stain removal, flavor and splatter control. </FONT></P></TD></TR></TABLE>
<P STYLE="font-size:6px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>

<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Fluorides.&nbsp;The Company has a variety of flavors of fluorides in gel formulation.&nbsp;Fluorides are used to help prevent tooth decay.
</FONT></P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">

<TD WIDTH="2%" VALIGN="top" ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">&#149;</FONT></TD>
<TD WIDTH="1%" VALIGN="top"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></TD>
<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Handpieces and components.&nbsp;Under the Athena Champion and Denticator brand names, the Company manufactures and markets low and high-speed dental
handpieces.&nbsp;Handpieces are used for teeth-cleaning and during restorative procedures, including removing decay during cavity preparation procedures.&nbsp;The Company also provides repair and maintenance services for handpieces.
</FONT></P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>

<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Endodontic.&nbsp;</I></B>The Company sells endodontic products under the Obtura and Spartan brand names.&nbsp;Endodontics is the part of dentistry
associated with the treatment of tooth root, dental pulp and surrounding tissue.&nbsp;The most common therapy in endodontics is the root canal procedure, which involves removing the organic root canal tissue and then filling the empty canal with
gutta percha, a rubber-like filling material.&nbsp;Endodontic procedures are performed by both general dentists and specialists (endodontists).&nbsp;The Company&#146;s endodontic products include: </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="font-size:6px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>
<TABLE STYLE="BORDER-COLLAPSE:COLLAPSE" BORDER="0" CELLPADDING="0" CELLSPACING="0" WIDTH="100%">

<TD ALIGN="left" VALIGN="top"> <P ALIGN="left"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Obturation.&nbsp;The Obtura family of endodontic units is gun-type, heat-softened gutta percha delivery system.&nbsp;This unique dispensing unit allows
the dentist to deliver a consistent flow of warm gutta percha directly into the canal, similar to extruding hot glue from a glue gun, facilitating the canal-filling procedure.&nbsp;Additionally, the Obtura system help practitioners effectively seal
the canal, which is an important component of the root canal procedure. </FONT></P></TD></TR></TABLE> <P STYLE="font-size:6px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>

</I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company markets its preventive, micro-applicator and infection control products to dental professionals worldwide primarily
through a network of non-exclusive relationships with dental product distributors. All major distributors of dental products in North America sell the Company&#146;s products, including Henry Schein, Inc. and Patterson Companies, Inc., which
accounted for 17.9% and 13.7%, respectively, of the Company&#146;s sales in 2011.&nbsp;The Company sells product to Henry Schein Inc., Patterson Companies Inc. and its other distributors upon the receipt of purchase orders.&nbsp;In addition to
selling through distributors in the U.S., the Company sells products directly to dental providers and dental hygiene schools, Veterans Administration healthcare facilities, and U.S. military bases. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company actively supports its distributor relationships with Company sales personnel and independent sales representatives in the U.S. and Canada as
well as in countries outside of North America.&nbsp;These sales representatives educate the Company&#146;s distributors about the quality, reliability and features of its products.&nbsp;The Company also advertises its products through industry
publications.&nbsp;To supplement its other marketing efforts, the Company provides product samples to dental professionals and exhibits its products at industry trade shows.&nbsp;In addition, the Company seeks to generate interest in its products by
providing information and marketing materials to influential lecturers and consultants in the dental industry. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company&#146;s diagnostic,
home care, endodontic and certain infection control products are marketed in the U.S. and Canada directly to the end user, primarily by direct mail, telemarketing, trade shows, and a limited amount of advertising in trade and professional
journals.&nbsp;The Company also sponsors seminars hosted by industry thought leaders. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Product Development </I></B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company&#146;s engineers and chemists are focused on developing innovative professional dental products and are actively involved in improving the
Company&#146;s manufacturing processes.&nbsp;Frequently, products are designed and developed in response to needs articulated to the Company by dental professionals.&nbsp;For example, the Company designed a short prophy cup for its line of prophy
angles to allow for easier access to the back of the patient&#146;s mouth.&nbsp;The Company holds various patents and trademarks but does not consider its business to be materially dependent upon any individual patent or trademark. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our commitment to internally-developed products requires an investment of resources.&nbsp;The Company incurred research and development costs which are
expensed as incurred totaling $1,906, $840 and $797, for 2011, 2010 and 2009, respectively. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company seeks to acquire product lines to
strengthen&nbsp;its market presence.&nbsp;During the first quarter of 2010, the Company acquired the rights to distribute a product line which expands our infection control offerings.</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Manufacturing and Supply </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company manufactures most of its products and product

STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Prophy Angles and Related Products.</I> The Company manufactures prophy angles and related products in its Earth City, Missouri facility.&nbsp;The primary processes involved in manufacturing the
Company&#146;s prophy angles and </FONT></P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">7 </FONT></P>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">
related products include precision metal turning and milling, rubber molding, plastic injection molding, component parts assembly and finished goods packaging.&nbsp;In these processes, the
Company uses a variety of computer numerically controlled (CNC) machining centers, injection molding machines and robotic assembly machines, and continues to invest in new and more efficient equipment and production lines. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Prophy Pastes.</I> The Company blends and mixes all of its pastes at the Earth City, Missouri facility. The Company also owns equipment used to form
and die-cut expanded polyethylene foam and extruded plastic into packaging materials, and equipment used to package its products in a variety of container sizes, including prophy paste in unit-dose containers. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Micro-applicators.&nbsp;</I>The Company manufactures micro-applicators and related products in its Grafton, Wisconsin and Dungarvan, Ireland
facilities.&nbsp;The primary processes involved in the manufacture of these products include plastic injection molding, application of adhesive and flocking, and packaging of finished goods.&nbsp;The Company uses injection molding machines and
robotic assembly machines in these manufacturing processes. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Handpieces and Components.&nbsp;</I>The Company uses a variety of CNC machines
to manufacture a number of the components required to produce its high-speed and low-speed handpieces in its Earth City, Missouri facility.&nbsp;Certain other handpiece component parts are sourced from a variety of Original Equipment Manufacturers
(OEMs).&nbsp;The Company assembles and provides repair services for its handpieces in its Earth City, Missouri facility. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Infection Control
Products.</I> The Company manufactures and packages a variety of infection control products, sterilants and cleaners, at the Earth City, Missouri facility.&nbsp;Additionally, certain of the Company&#146;s infection control products, including liquid
surface disinfectants and wipes, gloves, and masks, are sourced from domestic and international manufacturers. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Diagnostic Equipment.</I>
Diagnostic equipment is primarily manufactured and assembled at the Company&#146;s facility in Ft. Wayne, Indiana. Additionally, the Company also sources some diagnostic equipment and sensors for its digital diagnostic equipment from international
suppliers. The Company manufactures its own diagnostic generators, a critical system component for most of its diagnostic equipment. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Home
Care Products.</I>&nbsp;The Company sources most of its home care kit components and toothbrushes from manufacturers in Asia, principally China, Taiwan, Malaysia, India and Vietnam.&nbsp;Certain other toothbrush and toothpaste products are sourced
from domestic manufacturers. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Endodontic Products.</I>&nbsp;Obturation and ultrasonic systems are manufactured at the Algonquin, Illinois
facility.&nbsp;A variety of components and subassemblies are sourced domestically. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Supply.</I> The Company purchases a wide variety of raw
materials, including bar steel, brass, rubber and plastic resins from numerous suppliers. The majority of the Company's purchases are commodities readily available at competitive prices. As described above, the Company also purchases certain
additional products from other manufacturers for resale.&nbsp;The Company purchases its raw materials and other supplies and products through purchase orders and has entered into supply agreements with certain suppliers. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Competition </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The markets for the
Company&#146;s products are highly competitive.&nbsp;The Company believes that the principal competitive factors in all of its markets are product features, reliability, name recognition, established distribution network, customer service and, to a
lesser extent, price.&nbsp;The relative speed with which the Company can develop, complete testing, obtain regulatory approval and sell commercial quantities of new products is also an important competitive factor.&nbsp;Some of the Company&#146;s
competitors have greater financial, research, manufacturing, and marketing resources than the Company.&nbsp;The Company&#146;s competitors include: DENTSPLY International Inc.; Danaher Corporation; John O. Butler Company; Planmeca OY; StarDental, a
division of DentalEZ Group; Procter and Gamble Co. and Colgate-Palmolive Co. </FONT></P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">8 </FONT></P>


<p Style='page-break-before:always'>
<HR  SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">
<h5 align="left"><a href="#toc">Table of Contents</a></h5>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company competes with manufacturers of both branded and private-label dental products.&nbsp;The Company
believes it is a leading U.S. manufacturer or distributor of prophy angles and cups, liquid surface disinfectants, dental micro-applicators and obturation units designed for warm, vertical condensation. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>FDA Regulation </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The
Company&#146;s products are subject to regulation by, among other governmental entities, the U.S. Food and Drug Administration (&#147;FDA&#148;).&nbsp;To the extent the products are marketed abroad, they are also subject to government regulation in
the various foreign countries in which the products are produced or sold.&nbsp;Some of these regulatory requirements and penalties for non-compliance are more stringent than those applicable in the United States.&nbsp;They also vary from country to
country. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Medical Device Regulation</I>.&nbsp;Pursuant to the Federal Food, Drug, and Cosmetic Act (&#147;Act&#148;), and the FDA&#146;s
implementing regulations, FDA regulates the development, manufacture, sale, and distribution of medical devices, including their introduction into interstate commerce, as well as their testing, labeling, packaging, marketing, distribution,
recordkeeping and reporting.&nbsp;In general, if a dental device is subject to FDA regulation, compliance with the FDA requirements constitutes compliance with corresponding state regulations though some states may have additional licensure and
other requirements and may conduct inspections of facilities on behalf of or in addition to inspections by the FDA.&nbsp;Medical devices are classified for FDA regulatory purposes as Class I, Class II or Class III, depending on the degree of control
necessary to provide a reasonable assurance of safety and effectiveness for the labeled use.&nbsp;Currently, the Company&#146;s dental device products are classified as either Class I or Class II devices.&nbsp;Class I devices are subject to
&#147;general controls,&#148; such as labeling, good manufacturing practices (GMP), and a prohibition against adulteration and misbranding.&nbsp;Class II devices are subject to general and &#147;special controls,&#148; including premarket
notification (510(k)), and other general and device-specific requirements.&nbsp;All of the Company&#146;s dental device products are subject to ongoing regulatory oversight by the FDA to ensure compliance with, among other things, product labeling,
GMP/quality system regulation (QSR), recordkeeping, and medical device (adverse reaction) reporting requirements.&nbsp;The Company&#146;s medical imaging products as well as other products that emit radiation are also subject to additional FDA
requirements for radiation safety.&nbsp;The Company&#146;s facilities are further subject to periodic inspection by the FDA, as well as state and local agencies.&nbsp;Failure to satisfy FDA requirements can result in FDA enforcement actions,
including product seizure, injunction, fines and civil penalties and/or criminal or civil proceedings.&nbsp;In the medical device arena, the FDA may also request repair, replacement, or refund of the cost of any medical device manufactured or
distributed by the Company.&nbsp;The Company is also subject to state and local enforcement action and/or penalties, which may be greater than those imposed by the Act. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Drug Regulation</I>.&nbsp;The Company also markets drug products, such as fluorides, which are subject to regulation by the FDA and the counterpart agencies of the foreign countries where the products
are sold.&nbsp;The FDA regulates the development, manufacture, sale, and distribution of drugs, including their introduction into interstate commerce as well as their testing, labeling, packaging, marketing, distribution, recordkeeping and
reporting.&nbsp;In general, unless a drug product falls within an over-the-counter (OTC) monograph, is generally recognized as safe and effective for the labeled use, or is entitled to grandfather status, the drug must be approved by FDA pursuant to
an approved new drug application before it may be legally marketed.&nbsp;Drugs are also subject to post approval requirements such as adverse event reporting, record keeping and reporting requirements, cGMP manufacturing, advertising and promotion
requirements.&nbsp;Manufacturing facilities are subject to periodic and for cause inspections by FDA for compliance with FDA requirements.&nbsp;The Company also operates as a wholesale prescription drug distributor in some states and is required to
be licensed by individual states, subject to minimum FDA requirements and additional state requirements.&nbsp;Failure to comply with the FDA&#146;s drug regulatory requirements can result in issuance of an FDA Form 483 Inspectional Observations,
Warning Letter, and/or other civil or criminal enforcement action or penalty.&nbsp;The Company is also subject to state and local enforcement action and/or penalties, which may be greater than those imposed by the Act. </FONT></P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">9 </FONT></P>


<p Style='page-break-before:always'>
<HR  SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">
<h5 align="left"><a href="#toc">Table of Contents</a></h5>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Federal Insecticide, Fungicide, and Rodenticide Act (FIFRA) </I></B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Certain of our infection control products are classified as pesticides and are subject to regulation by the United States Environmental Protection Agency
(EPA) under FIFRA, and by various state agencies under the laws of those states.&nbsp;Generally, under FIFRA and state law, no one may sell or distribute a pesticide unless it is registered with the EPA and each state in which the product is
sold.&nbsp;Registrations must be renewed annually.&nbsp;Registration includes approval by the EPA of the product&#146;s label, including its claims and directions for use, which must be supported by data.&nbsp;EPA or states may at any time require
additional testing to determine whether a pesticide could cause adverse effects on the environment, including people, and whether it is efficacious.&nbsp;The pesticide laws also require registrants to report adverse effects associated with their
products to the EPA and the corresponding state agency and impose certain recordkeeping and reporting requirements.&nbsp;Failure to pay annual registration fees or to provide necessary testing data, or new information regarding adverse effects of
product, as well as other conduct, could result in fines and/or the suspension or cancellation of a pesticide registration.&nbsp;The Company&#146;s facilities are subject to periodic inspection by the EPA, as well as state and local
agencies.&nbsp;Failure to satisfy EPA requirements can result in EPA enforcement actions, including stop sale, use or removal orders, fines and civil penalties and/or criminal or civil proceedings.&nbsp;The Company is also subject to state and local
enforcement action and/or penalties, which may be greater than those imposed by FIFRA.&nbsp;To the extent the products are manufactured or marketed abroad, they may also be subject to government regulation in the various foreign countries in which
the products are produced or sold.&nbsp;Some of these regulatory requirements and penalties for noncompliance may be more stringent than those applicable in the United States.&nbsp;They also vary from country to country. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Environmental, Health and Safety Matters </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our operations involve production processes and the use and handling of materials that are subject to federal, state, and local environmental laws and regulations relating to, among other things, solid
and hazardous waste disposal, air emissions, and waste water discharge.&nbsp;We are also required to comply with federal and state laws and regulations relating to occupational health and safety.&nbsp;If violations of any of these laws and
regulations occur, or if toxic or hazardous materials are released into the environment as a result of our operations, the Company could be exposed to significant liability. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company believes it is in compliance in all material respects with respect to the laws and regulations applicable to it and its operations. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Other </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">During the third quarter of 2011, the Company entered into a new credit
facility agreement. The new credit facility increased the aggregate commitment from $60,000 to $70,000 and expires July 2016.&nbsp;As of December&nbsp;31, 2011, the Company had $650 in outstanding borrowings under this agreement and $69,350
available for borrowing.&nbsp;The Company expects to fund working capital requirements from a combination of available cash balances and internally generated funds, and from the borrowing arrangement mentioned above. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Some seasonality exists in the business driven by timing of price increases, rebate incentives, tax incentives, and holiday buying patterns and
promotions. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Employees </I></B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">As of December&nbsp;31, 2011, the Company employed 371 people, none of whom were covered by collective bargaining agreements.&nbsp;The Company believes
its relations with its employees are good. </FONT></P>

<P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Acquisitions have been and continue to be an important part of our growth strategy; failure to consummate acquisitions could limit our growth and
failure to successfully integrate acquisitions could adversely impact our results. </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our business strategy includes continued growth
through strategic acquisitions, which depends upon our ability to identify potential acquisitions, the availability of suitable acquisition candidates at reasonable prices and the availability of financing on acceptable terms.&nbsp;In addition, our
growth strategy is dependent upon our ability to negotiate and consummate acquisitions and effectively integrate the acquisitions into our business.&nbsp;Acquisitions involve integrating product lines, employees and manufacturing facilities and
include risks relating to employee turnover and sales channel tension.&nbsp;We cannot be certain that we will successfully manage all of these challenges and risks in the future and there can be no assurance that our future acquisitions will be
successful.&nbsp;Failure to consummate appropriate acquisitions would adversely impact our growth and failure to successfully integrate them would adversely affect our results. </FONT></P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">12 </FONT></P>



<p Style='page-break-before:always'>
<HR  SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">
<h5 align="left"><a href="#toc">Table of Contents</a></h5>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>We operate in a highly competitive industry and cannot be certain that we will be able to compete
effectively. </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The markets for our products are highly competitive. We compete with manufacturers of both branded and private-label
dental products.&nbsp;Some of our competitors have greater financial, research, manufacturing, marketing and other resources which could allow them to compete more successfully.&nbsp;As a result we may not be able to achieve, maintain or increase
market share or margins, or compete effectively against these companies.&nbsp;In addition, new competitors are constantly entering the markets in which we participate. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>We rely heavily on key distributors, and if any of them stop doing business with us it would significantly impact our operating results. </I></B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">In fiscal 2011, approximately 31.6% of our sales were made through two distributors.&nbsp;If either of these two distributors were to materially change
the timing of their order patterns, our financial results in any given short-term period could be adversely affected.&nbsp;Additionally, the loss of either distributor as a customer, or a material change in our relationship with either distributor
would have a material adverse effect on our results of operations and financial condition until we find an alternative means to distribute our products. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>The strength of the dental market is impacted by general worldwide economic conditions. </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Uncertain or negative economic conditions, along with higher unemployment, adversely affect the dental market in general.&nbsp;We cannot predict the timing or duration of any economic slowdown or the
timing or strength of a subsequent economic recovery.</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>If we are unable to successfully manage growth, our results could be materially
adversely affected. </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company is committed to growth in part through acquisition as well as organically through the development of
new products.&nbsp;Our commitment to growth will continue to place a significant strain on our management, administrative, operational, and financial resources. In order to address these concerns, the Company is focused on attracting and retaining
key employees who possess outstanding talent for achieving short and long-term objectives.&nbsp;We will also have to continue to consolidate our operations and improve and upgrade operational, financial and accounting systems, controls and
infrastructure as well as expand, train and manage our employees. Our failure to manage the future expansion of our business could have a material adverse effect on our revenues and profitability. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Changes in standard of care relating to dental health may impact future results. </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The demand for dental products is impacted by the dental health care standards.&nbsp;If the industry standards were to shift to a standard of care that changed the frequency or type of procedures
performed or if third-party insurance coverage were to substantially change, the market for our Company&#146;s products could be adversely affected. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Changes in the healthcare industry may impact future results. </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The healthcare
industry is in the process of undergoing significant changes.&nbsp;The Patient Protection and Affordable Care Act (PPACA), which was signed into law in March 2010, contains legislation that increases the number of people covered by
insurance.&nbsp;As this legislation continues to evolve, it is difficult to predict how this will ultimately impact the dental industry and our business. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Our business is subject to quarterly variations in operating results due to factors outside of our control. </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our business is subject to quarterly variations in operating results caused by a number of factors, including business and industry conditions, the timing of acquisitions, the buying patterns of our
largest customers, technology changes, currency exchange rate fluctuations, and other factors beyond our control. All these factors make it difficult to predict operating results for any particular period. </FONT></P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">13 </FONT></P>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Our future performance is materially dependent upon our senior management. </I></B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our future success is substantially dependent upon the efforts and abilities of members of our existing senior management, particularly Alfred E. Brennan,
Chairman and Chief Executive Officer, and Arthur L. Herbst, Jr., President and Chief Financial Officer, among others. The loss of the services of either Mr.&nbsp;Brennan or Mr.&nbsp;Herbst could have a material adverse effect on our business. We
have employment agreements with each of Messrs. Brennan and Herbst.&nbsp;We do not currently have &#147;key man&#148; life insurance policies on any of our employees. Competition for senior management is intense, and we may not be successful in
attracting and retaining key personnel. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Difficulty in obtaining goods and services from our vendors and suppliers could adversely
affect our business. </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">We are dependent on certain vendors and suppliers.&nbsp;We believe we have good relationships with and are
generally able to obtain attractive pricing and other terms from our vendors and suppliers.&nbsp;However, if our raw material products, such as resin, were to increase in price significantly, the cost of manufacturing products would increase and our
profitability may be impacted.&nbsp;Our product offering includes a multitude of products supplied by a number of suppliers. When possible, we attempt to have multiple sources for key materials and supplies.&nbsp;However, some items are single
sourced. Although we believe there are alternative suppliers for the products we single source, if our vendors and suppliers were no longer able to or decided not to supply certain products, there could be delay in identifying and transitioning to
alternative suppliers, as well as increased costs, any of which could adversely affect our business for a period of time. If we are unable to identify and secure reasonably priced alternative suppliers in a timely manner, or if we encounter delays
or difficulty in obtaining raw materials or products from third-party suppliers, our business and results of operations could be adversely affected. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Certain of our products and manufacturing facilities are subject to regulation, and our failure or the failure of our suppliers to comply with applicable laws and regulations and obtain or maintain
the required regulatory approvals for these products could hinder or prevent their sale and increase our costs of regulatory compliance.&nbsp;In addition, there can be no assurance we will be able to successfully reintroduce any products that may
have been recalled. </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our ability to continue manufacturing and selling those of our products that are subject to regulation by the
United States Food and Drug Administration, the Environmental Protection Agency, state, local or foreign laws or other federal, state or foreign governmental bodies or agencies is subject to a number of risks, including our ability to comply with
existing laws and regulations and ongoing requirements, the promulgation of stricter laws or regulations, or the withdrawal of the approval needed to sell one or more of our products.&nbsp;We are also subject to regulation by the Occupational Safety
and Health Administration and the Securities and Exchange Commission, among others.</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">With respect to certain products we purchase, our ability
to sell such products to our customers is dependent upon the supplier complying with all applicable laws and regulations.&nbsp;The failure of the Company and/or our suppliers to comply with existing or future laws and regulations could prevent the
sale of certain products and have a material adverse effect on our operations.&nbsp;The costs of complying with these types of regulations and the delays in receiving required regulatory approvals or new laws, regulations, or regulatory requirements
adverse to our business may force the Company to discontinue selling products, make expensive changes to be able to continue to market existing products, make it more expensive and possibly prohibitive to introduce new products, require the Company
to recall products, increase our costs of regulatory compliance or hinder our growth.&nbsp;To the extent that we recall products, there can be no assurance that we will be able to timely and successfully reintroduce any products that may have been
recalled into the market.</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">As part of the federal legislation to reform the U.S healthcare system, a 2.3 percent excise tax on medical devices
is scheduled to be implemented in 2013 that will apply to U.S. sales of a majority of our medical device products.&nbsp;Accordingly, the Company is continuing to assess the financial impact of the new law on our business. </FONT></P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">14 </FONT></P>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Technological change could result in loss of market share, product obsolescence and dependence on new
products. </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our future success depends on the ability to anticipate and adapt to changes in technology, industry standards and
customers&#146; changing demands.&nbsp;If we do not timely acquire, develop, complete testing, obtain regulatory approval and sell commercial quantities of new products in response to a technological change or the changing demands of customers, we
could lose market share and our products could become obsolete. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Loss of intellectual property protection and related disputes could
negatively affect our results. </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Our Company utilizes copyright, trademark and trade secret laws, licenses and confidentiality and
non-disclosure agreements to protect our intellectual property and proprietary technology.&nbsp;There can be no assurance that the steps taken to protect our intellectual property or proprietary technology will be adequate or enforceable to prevent
misappropriation.&nbsp;In addition, the cost of prosecuting and defending such actions can be significant and could have a material adverse effect on our results. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>We rely upon others to assist in the education and promotion of our products, and the loss of the participation of these individuals could adversely affect our net sales.&nbsp;Changes in the rules
governing promotion of our products might likewise adversely affect our sales. </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">We work with various dental clinicians to educate the
dental community on the features and benefits of our products. Some clinicians are a key component to our strategy for growing our sales. The inability or unwillingness of one or more of these clinicians to continue to assist us could negatively
impact the sales of certain significant products.&nbsp;If new laws are enacted or new regulations promulgated that restrict promotional communications about our products, this may likewise adversely affect our sales. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>We are subject to potential product liability claims as a result of the design, manufacture and marketing of our products.</I></B> </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Claims alleging product liability may involve large potential damages and significant defense costs.&nbsp;We currently maintain insurance coverage for
such claims but there can be no assurance that our insurance coverage will be adequate or that all such claims will be covered by our insurance.&nbsp;A product liability claim could adversely affect our reputation.&nbsp;A successful claim against us
in excess of the available insurance coverage could have a material adverse effect on our results. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Changes in tax legislation could
impact the Company&#146;s effective tax rate and earnings. </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company is subject to federal, state, local and foreign
taxes.&nbsp;Changes in legislation, as well as operational and structural changes in the Company&#146;s business, could affect the Company&#146;s effective tax rate, which could ultimately impact our net earnings.&nbsp;Additionally, the Company is
subject to federal, state, and local tax audits, as well as audits by non-US tax authorities.&nbsp;The audits could result in unfavorable tax adjustments, which could negatively impact the Company&#146;s results. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Operating results could be impacted by an impairment to the Company&#146;s goodwill or other intangible assets. </I></B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">At least annually, the Company is required to perform an impairment review on goodwill and intangible assets under US GAAP.&nbsp;Various assumptions are
used in the company&#146;s analysis, including royalty rates, sales growth rates and dental industry growth rates.&nbsp;The company may be required to record a charge to earnings based upon the outcome of the review. </FONT></P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">15 </FONT></P>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Quality or safety concerns could put the Company at risk for product recalls. </I></B></FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company may need to recall products for quality or safety reasons.&nbsp;While the Company&#146;s products generally have a good reputation with
customers and end-users, product safety or quality concerns could result in potential governmental action or litigation.&nbsp;The Company&#146;s reputation or financial results could be impacted by publicity or costs related to company, governmental
or legal action. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Our international operations could expose the Company to intrinsic risks that have the potential to affect our
business. </I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Uncertainties in our international operations could adversely affect our business, results of operations and financial
condition. These risks include, among other items, fluctuations in currency exchange rates, longer customer payment cycles, regulatory requirements, import or export requirements, repatriation of cash from our foreign operations, and economic and
political instability. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><I>Our reliance on information systems could adversely affect the continued operations of the Company.
</I></B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">We rely on information systems in our business to process and manage data to help us: facilitate the purchase and distribution of our
inventory items; receive, process, and ship orders; manage the accurate billing of customers; and process payments to suppliers. There can be no assurances that we can prevent breakdowns or breaches within our system that could ultimately have a
significant adverse effect on the business operations and financial condition of the Company. As we upgrade and evolve our computer systems and software, we may also encounter issues that could have an adverse effect on the overall business.

<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company has paid quarterly dividends on its Common Stock since the third quarter of 2003.&nbsp;In addition to the quarterly cash dividend of $.04 per
share, the Company issued a special cash dividend of $1.00 per share in the fourth quarter of 2011 and 2010. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Payment of future cash dividends
will be at the discretion of the Company's Board of Directors and will be dependent upon the earnings and financial condition of the Company and any other factors deemed relevant by the Board of Directors, and will also be subject to any applicable
restrictions contained in the Company's then existing credit arrangements. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Issuer Purchases of Equity Securities (in thousands, except per

</TABLE> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px; margin-left:7%; text-indent:-7%"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B><A NAME="tx274150_9"></A>Item&nbsp;7. Management's Discussion and Analysis of
Financial Condition and Results of Operation (in thousands). </B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>General </B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Young Innovations, Inc. and its subsidiaries (the &#147;Company&#148;) develops, manufactures and markets supplies and equipment used by dentists, dental hygienists, dental assistants and
patients.&nbsp;The Company's product offering includes disposable and metal prophylaxis (&#147;prophy&#148;) angles, prophy cups and brushes, dental micro-applicators, panoramic X-ray machines, moisture control products, infection control products,
dental handpieces (drills) and related components, endodontic systems, orthodontic toothbrushes, flavored examination gloves, children's toothbrushes, and children's toothpastes. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company&#146;s manufacturing and distribution facilities are located in Missouri, Illinois, California, Indiana, Wisconsin and Ireland. </FONT></P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">18 </FONT></P>



<p Style='page-break-before:always'>
<HR  SIZE="3" style="COLOR:#999999" WIDTH="100%" ALIGN="CENTER">
<h5 align="left"><a href="#toc">Table of Contents</a></h5>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company operates in one reporting segment, which is the development and manufacture of a broad line of
products marketed to dental professionals.&nbsp;The Company markets its products primarily in the U.S.&nbsp;The Company also markets its products in several international markets, including: Canada, Europe, South America, Central America, and the
Pacific Rim.&nbsp;Sales outside the United States represented approximately 17%, 18%, and 17% of the Company&#146;s total net sales in 2011, 2010, and 2009, respectively. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">Some of the risk factors that affect the Company&#146;s business and financial results are discussed in &#147;Item 1A: Risk&nbsp;Factors.&#148;&nbsp;We wish to caution the reader that the risk factors
discussed in &#147;Item 1A: Risk Factors&#148;, and those described elsewhere in this report or our other Securities and Exchange Commission filings, could cause our actual results to differ materially from those stated in the forward-looking
statements. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Critical Accounting Policies </B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The SEC has requested that all registrants include in their MD&amp;A a description of their most critical accounting policies, the judgments and uncertainties affecting the application of those policies,
and the likelihood that materially different amounts would be reported under different conditions using different assumptions.&nbsp;The SEC indicated that a &#147;critical accounting policy&#148; is one which is both important to the portrayal of
the company&#146;s financial condition and results and requires management&#146;s most difficult, subjective or complex judgments, often as a result of the need to make estimates about the effect of matters that are inherently uncertain.&nbsp;The
Company believes that the following accounting policies fit this definition: </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Revenue Recognition</I> &#150; Revenue from the sale of
products is recorded at the time title passes, generally when the products are shipped, as the Company&#146;s shipping terms are customarily FOB shipping point.</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company generally requires payment within 30 days from the date of invoice and offers cash discounts for early payment.&nbsp;For certain acquired businesses that offer different terms, the Company
migrates these customers to the terms referred above over time. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The Company offers discounts to its distributors if certain conditions are
met.&nbsp;Customer allowances, volume discounts and rebates, and other short-term incentive programs are recorded as a reduction in reported revenues at the time of sale.&nbsp;The Company reduces product revenue for the estimated redemption of
annual rebates on certain current product sales. Customer allowances and rebates are estimated based on historical experience and known trends. </FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The policy with respect to sales returns generally provides that a customer may not return inventory except at the Company&#146;s option.&nbsp;The
Company generally warrants its products against defects, and its most generous policy provides a two-year parts and labor warranty on X-ray equipment.&nbsp;The Company owns X-ray equipment rented on a month-to-month basis to customers.</FONT></P>
<P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Allowance for doubtful accounts</I> &#150; The Company has 39% of its December&nbsp;31, 2011 accounts receivable balance with two large customers (see
footnote 4 of the notes to consolidated financial statements set forth in Item&nbsp;8) with the remaining balance comprised of amounts due from numerous customers, some of which are international.&nbsp;Accounts receivable balances are subject to
credit risk.&nbsp;Management has reserved for expected credit losses, sales returns and allowances, and discounts based upon past experience, as well as knowledge of current customer information.&nbsp;The Company believes that its reserves are
adequate.&nbsp;It is possible, however, that the accuracy of our estimation process could be impacted by unforeseen circumstances.&nbsp;The Company continuously reviews its reserve balance and refines the estimates to reflect any changes in
circumstances. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Inventory</I> &#150; The Company values inventory at the lower of cost or market on a first-in, first-out (FIFO)
basis.&nbsp;Inventory values are based upon standard costs, which approximate actual costs.&nbsp;Management regularly reviews inventory quantities on hand and records a write-down for excess or obsolete inventory based primarily on estimated product
demand and other information related to the inventory including planned introduction of </FONT></P>
 <p STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">19 </FONT></P>



<p Style='page-break-before:always'>

<h5 align="left"><a href="#toc">Table of Contents</a></h5>

 <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">
new products and changes in technology.&nbsp;If demand for the Company&#146;s products is significantly different than management&#146;s expectations, the value of inventory could be materially
impacted.&nbsp;Inventory write-downs are included in cost of goods sold. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Goodwill and other intangible assets</I> &#150; In accordance
with Accounting Standards Codification (&#147;ASC&#148;)&nbsp;Topic 350, &#147;Intangibles- Goodwill and Other,&#148; goodwill and other intangible assets with indefinite useful lives are reviewed by management for impairment at least annually, or
whenever events or changes in circumstances indicate the carrying amount may not be recoverable.&nbsp;If indicators of impairment are present, the determination of the amount of impairment would be based on management&#146;s judgment as to the
future operating cash flows to be generated from the assets.&nbsp;ASC Topic 350 also requires that intangible assets with estimable useful lives be amortized over their respective estimated useful lives to their estimated residual values, and
reviewed for impairment in accordance with ASC Topic 360, &#147;Property, Plant, and Equipment.&#148; </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Stock compensation</I> &#150; In
accordance with ASC Topic 718, &#147;Stock Compensation,&#148; compensation cost is recognized within the financial statements at the grant date based on the award&#146;s fair-value.&nbsp;Stock option fair value is calculated by an option pricing
model, and is recognized as expense ratably over the requisite service period. The option pricing models require judgmental assumptions including: volatility, forfeiture rates, and expected option life. If any of the assumptions used in the model
change significantly, share-based compensation expense may differ in the future from that recorded in the current period.&nbsp;The Company uses the Black-Scholes option pricing model.&nbsp;Compensation expense is also recognized for restricted stock
using the fair market value of our Common Stock at the date of grant. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:12px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><I>Assets Acquired and Liabilities Assumed in Business Combinations</I>
&#150; The Company periodically acquires businesses.&nbsp;The Company accounts for business acquisitions under the provisions of ASC Topic 805, &#147;Business Combinations,&#148; which requires an entity to recognize the assets acquired, liabilities
assumed, contractual contingencies, and contingent consideration at their fair value on the acquisition date.&nbsp;It further requires that acquisition-related costs be recognized separately from the acquisition and expensed as incurred,
restructuring costs generally expensed in the periods subsequent to the acquisition date, and changes in accounting for deferred tax asset valuation allowances and acquired income tax uncertainties after the measurement period impact income tax
expense.&nbsp;In addition, acquired in-process research and development is capitalized as an intangible asset and amortized over its estimated useful life.</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:18px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT
STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2"><B>Results of Operations </B></FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:6px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">The following table sets forth, for the periods indicated, certain
items from the Company's statements of income expressed as a percentage of net sales. </FONT></P> <P STYLE="font-size:12px;margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px">&nbsp;</P>

 <p STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px"><FONT SIZE="1">&nbsp;</FONT></P> <P STYLE="margin-top:0px;margin-bottom:0px" ALIGN="center"><FONT STYLE="font-family:Times New Roman" SIZE="2">20 </FONT></P>
